# Hardware data archive manifest

The raw hardware telemetry (CSV time series and settle logs) lives
in the operator's private data archive OUTSIDE this repository;
paths below are relative to that archive's root. The small
`.dwells.json` sidecars — the tuning records, dwell verdicts, and
demodulated estimates the documentation quotes — are tracked
in-repo under `data/` (one per starred file below). Checksums are
SHA-256 at the archival date stated by each section; the older tables
below were archived on 2026-07-28. The control-rate sections (`e0/`,
`e1_prod/`, `e1_cal/` and `r4_bounded/`, checksums taken 2026-11-04;
`b2b3_bounded/`, taken 2026-11-05; `traced_torque_off/`, `latency_timer/`
and `profiled_torque_off/`, taken 2026-11-06; `timer_rehearsal/`, an
emulator rehearsal, taken 2026-11-07; `timer_torque_off/`, taken 2026-11-08;
`stage1_rehearsal/`, emulator rehearsals, taken 2026-11-09 and, for attempts
10 and 11, 2026-11-10; `stage1_hardware/`, taken 2026-11-10;
`clock_validation/`, an emulator validation, taken 2026-11-11)
are kept on a separate volume of the
same private archive; their paths are
relative to that volume's project folder. A citation in the project records
is verifiable by checking the named file against its hash. Project
records and code comments cite these datasets by
bare filename (e.g. `track8.csv`, `pass1.csv`, `p1_j1_survey_r2.csv`)
— every such name resolves to a row in the tables below. The exception
is a name two sections share: six untraced and traced runs of 2026-11-06
have the same names in `traced_torque_off/` and `profiled_torque_off/`
(different runs, different hashes), so those runs and their sidecars are
cited with their directory, as in
`profiled_torque_off/rate-trace-current-1000hz-rt-loaded-bounded-fast-motion-traced-20261106-1.csv.run.txt`.
The session tools refuse a log name already listed here.

## `gravity-calibration/` — M-GC3 calibrated float tests (2026-07-30)

Evidence of the gravity-calibration remediation's hardware milestone
(`docs/records/history.md`, M-GC3): the vendor-scale
configuration, marker-first release, displacement-bounded acceptance.

| file | role | sha256 |
|---|---|---|
| `float2.csv` | M-GC3 acceptance run: PASSED all four preregistered conditions (peak speed 0.024 rad/s, displacement 0.0123 rad, zero gates/clamps, final-second drift ≤ 0.0015 rad); marker 4.080 s, window complete | `de1f1a3b333b26534b5617d5494a9c194e6e9c4555e32fcbeb81c7b02141fbf1` |
| `float3.csv` | M-GC3 back-drive feel-check run (operator back-driving during the window — NOT acceptance evidence): reported distinctly lighter than the 2026-07-29 failed run; zero gates/clamps, 503/503 accepted | `f09057f289066be6d60a9154c97f71ba141e7daccd8b993670ba0df3da7726fd` |
| `feel_j1_diag1.csv` | Reviewer-authorized gate-free j1 diagnostic session (2026-07-31, v2 instrumented log: event marks + raw counts; NOT acceptance evidence): VALID — `--feel --gate-free` passes, 6001 rows, 9 operator event marks, zero clamps/gates on every axis. Findings: host goal smooth (max 4 counts/cycle over the whole run); static current delivery clean (stationary error ≤ 7 counts at goals up to ~300 counts); all 93 present-current steps > 30 counts (max 73 ≈ 0.47 Nm nominal) occurred DURING motion, none stationary — breakaway/reversal transients; the two most violent in-window transients coincide with the 31.15 s / 34.93 s notch marks | `cd7a2ddac280e2b313213e6c6fc789849e4930c51c98d9398494a755ecf2ba6f` |
| `feel_j0.csv` … `feel_j6.csv` | M-GC3 per-joint back-drive sessions (feel-check mode, one target joint each, bidirectional coverage log-confirmed on every target; NOT acceptance evidence). Reviewer disposition (2026-07-30, supersedes the preliminary stiction attribution): subjective feel FAILED on j1 (random notchiness; power-off check clean, so torque-ENABLED — suspects XM540 current regulation / load-dependent drivetrain friction / cross-axis j3 gate discontinuities (18 gated cycles in the j1 session, ≈1.01 Nm zeroed ~33.6 s while j1 was stationary — uncorrelatable, no event marks), NOT the gravity model: the trajectory replay agrees within ≈5 % near the high-command extreme; lower-load trajectories show amplitude ratios differing ~10–12 % with small absolute differences) and inconclusive/failed on j4 (hand-guided positive tendency; likely compensation-induced — replay shows rtctrl at ~1.4× the vendor model's twist amplitude, within 1–3 goal counts); j3/j6 pass (symmetric rigidity). Zero clamps, all submissions accepted; position gates by axis: j0 92 / j3 18 (j1 session) / j4 853 / j6 91 cycles (endpoint dwell) | j0 `290b95c786003e7a0c5666c65749a85d9a992d16457dfca16a02ee118f3c5ec6` j1 `d33b607c68c3a8eb2a2c4b354e69ffb56c43ddf674725aca8b92143dd7aedda8` j2 `b01a7299cf77fed9710097175530d3275afad39eacbb7d17d244e98f9bf45e73` j3 `2a5901b2dcad29499ae49d809112bb8977dc22c56b83ef4d387d4cefc1219316` j4 `b8af89f405ec5e4d668a90da14b6a1c2614ddaa035ff49667d454253712b6327` j5 `1ae7c471a86455d90937e864a6c963313147ddc66e60959919fdde23cb37fb26` j6 `3902dbcc17fc7c9b8aacb2577e2bc143da767ddbb8c660071ec1277407a565b6` |

## `follow-cbp-gain-sweep-20260827/` — servo-side gain sweep (2026-08-27)

Complete x7_follow simulation and hardware bundles for the position versus
current-based position comparison and the subsequent CBP gain sweep. The
archive also contains motor dumps, derived comparison plots, all intermediate
TOML configurations, and patches recovered from the reflog after the accidental
hard reset. The selected tuning and rejected alternatives are summarized in
[history.md](history.md#current-based-position-gain-decision-2026-08-27).

## `e1_prod/` — control-rate E1 production traffic, legacy read path (2026-11-03)

33 torque-off probe runs of 60 s (`tools/e1_prod.sh` at `dba323d`), three files
each; the climbs stopped at the first clearly failing rate, so there is no
1000 Hz run and no loaded 500 Hz run. Exploration only, never acceptance
evidence; cited in [history.md](history.md#control-rate-exploration-e0-and-e1-2026-11-03).

## `e1_cal/` — control-rate E1 calibration, legacy read path (2026-11-03)

Attempt 1 (two runs, `tools/e1_cal.sh` at `dba323d`) compared goal
read-backs, which the torque-off servos overwrite; attempt 2 (11 runs, at
`a16ff42`) acknowledges each write with a Model Number read. Overruns are
expected in calibration runs and never judged.

## `b2b3_bounded/` — control-rate B2/B3 torque-off runs on the bounded read path (2026-11-05)

295 runs at `630df10` with the 35 ms budget and an equal guard, three files
per run: for each of five read variants (the motion-only read with an
auxiliary read every 100 ms or none after the initial one, Fast Sync Read of
the full window, and Fast Sync Read with each motion-only cadence; `-motion`,
`-motion-aux0ms`, `-fast`, `-fast-motion` and `-fast-motion-aux0ms` in the
names), 48 production-traffic runs (`tools/e1_prod.sh --read-path bounded
--no-climb`) and 11 calibration runs (`tools/e1_cal.sh --read-path bounded
--rt-priority 80 --rt-cpu 3`). The evidence for the B2 and B3 measurements;
cited in [history.md](history.md#control-rate-b2-and-b3-on-hardware-2026-11-05).

## `traced_torque_off/` — control-rate traced torque-off diagnostics (2026-11-06)

14 runs at `b593b4d` (`tools/trace_session.sh` defaults, 60 s each): current
at 500 and 1000 Hz under rt-idle, rt-loaded and rt-loaded-excl, and cbp at
1000 Hz under rt-idle, each untraced and then traced, on the bounded read
path with the 35 ms budget and an equal guard. Each run has its per-cycle
log, probe summary, session record and task snapshot; a traced run also has
its trace and the adapter's usbmon capture. The evidence for the traced
diagnostics; cited in
[history.md](history.md#control-rate-traced-torque-off-diagnostics-2026-11-06).
Six of its names (current at 500 and 1000 Hz under rt-loaded, and at
1000 Hz under rt-loaded-excl, untraced and traced) recur in
`profiled_torque_off/` for different runs: cite those, and their sidecars,
with the directory.

## `latency_timer/` — control-rate adapter latency timer experiment, phase 1 (2026-11-06)

Two sessions at `50e1169` (`tools/trace_session.sh --latency-steps`) on
current at 500 Hz, rt-idle, with Fast Sync Read and the motion-only read:
attempt 1 with the steps 1, 0 and 1 ms (both 1 ms runs, and the 0 ms run
that stopped at the probe's startup inventory), and attempt 2, a traced
repetition of the 0 ms step; then the hold diagnostic at `d761371`
(`tools/latency_hold.py`, `rate-hold-20261106-1`). The evidence for the
phase-1 and hold results; cited in
[history.md](history.md#control-rate-traced-torque-off-diagnostics-2026-11-06).

## `profiled_torque_off/` — control-rate profiled torque-off matrix (2026-11-06)

9 runs at `c766bec` (`tools/trace_session.sh --modes untraced,traced,profiled`,
60 s each): current at 1000 Hz under rt-loaded and rt-loaded-excl and at
500 Hz under rt-loaded, each untraced, traced and profiled, on the bounded
read path with the 35 ms budget and an equal guard. Each run has its
per-cycle log, probe summary, session record and task snapshot; a traced or
profiled run also has its trace and the adapter's usbmon capture, and a
profiled run perf's samples of the real-time CPU and the worker's two
exports. The 500 Hz rt-loaded untraced and traced runs had an uncontrolled
background load (`exiftool` in their task snapshots). The 500 Hz and the
1000 Hz rt-loaded-excl profiles report lost records, so their sampling is
degraded; their trace timing stands. The six untraced and traced names
repeat names in `traced_torque_off/` for different runs (the session saw
only its output directory): cite them, and their sidecars, with the
directory. The evidence for the profiled diagnostics; cited in
[history.md](history.md#control-rate-traced-torque-off-diagnostics-2026-11-06).

| file | role | sha256 |

## `timer_rehearsal/` — control-rate timer-mode emulator rehearsal (2026-11-07)

The timer service diagnostics' rehearsal over `dxl_emu` (emulated servos on
a pty: no servo and no USB adapter), at `bc8413b`
(`tools/trace_session.sh --modes untraced,timer --exerciser`, current at
1000 Hz, rt-idle, 10 s). Emulator data, not hardware telemetry, kept with
checksums at the reviewer's request for the corrected full-run analysis.
Attempt 1 was refused by the probe before its run (the emulated servos at
the factory Return Delay Time 250): its session record and task snapshot
only. Attempt 2 holds the untraced control and the timer run with its trace,
TSC-clock instance, sampler pairs, SMI counts, exerciser log and process
listing. For attempt 2 the operator stopped `unattended-upgrades.service`
and `localsearch-3.service` by hand (15:21:47 to 15:22:39 JST) and restored
them; no upgrade ran then (the shutdown helper's log) and no indexer process
appears in the run's listings. The folder also holds a `SHA256SUMS` of
these files. Described in
[control-rate.md](../hardware/control-rate.md#timer-service-diagnostics-design-torque-off-1-ms).

## `timer_torque_off/` — control-rate timer-mode torque-off runs (2026-11-08)

The four targeted timer service runs on the arm, at `adc6143`
(`tools/trace_session.sh --modes untraced,timer`, no exerciser, 60 s each):
current at 500 Hz under rt-loaded and at 1000 Hz under rt-loaded-excl, each
an untraced control and then a timer run, on the bounded read path with the
35 ms budget and an equal guard, every servo torque-off. Each run has its
per-cycle log, probe summary, session record and task snapshot; a timer run
also has its process listing, timer-mode trace, TSC-clock trace instance,
sampler pairs, SMI counts and the adapter's usbmon capture. The operator
quieted the host with `tools/quiet_host.sh` (stopped at 14:10:48 JST,
restored after the session; its record is in every session record). The
1000 Hz timer run's main trace lost 48,404 events on CPU 3, so that run's
timer service evidence is incomplete. Attempt 2 repeats that pair at
`8493d14` at 20 s each (reviewer decision), an untraced control and a timer
run, every servo torque-off, the host quieted the same way (stopped at
16:12:45 JST, restored after the session); neither of its buffers lost
events. The folder also holds a `SHA256SUMS` of these files, attempt 2's
14 appended after the first 28 lines, which are unchanged. Described in
[control-rate.md](../hardware/control-rate.md#timer-service-diagnostics-design-torque-off-1-ms).

## `stage1_rehearsal/` — control-rate Stage 1 emulator rehearsals (2026-11-08 to 2026-11-10)

The fair-server instrumentation's and observer placement's rehearsals over
`dxl_emu` (emulated servos on a pty: no servo and no USB adapter), current
at 1000 Hz, rt-idle, 10 s per run (50 s in attempts 10 and 11), every run
torque-off. Emulator data, not
hardware telemetry, kept with checksums at the reviewer's request, failed
captures included. Each subfolder keeps its session directory's name and
holds a `SHA256SUMS` of its files. Described in
[control-rate.md](../hardware/control-rate.md).

**Failed or superseded captures (not accepted):**

- `stage1-controls/` (attempt 1, at `dcb3244`): the probe controls, an
  untraced control and a timer run whose old return probe caused 56,712
  NULL-page kernel faults; superseded.
- `stage1-controls-2/` (attempt 3, at `3a2d12d`): the controls, which pass,
  an untraced control and a timer run that lost 108,445 events on CPU 3.
- `stage1-placement-2/` (attempt 4, at `3a2d12d`): open and confined
  placements, each an untraced control and a timer run; the open timer run
  is complete in events but fails the SMI coverage rule, and the confined
  one lost 2,674,161 events on CPU 3.
- Attempt 2 (`stage1-placement`) was refused before writing anything and
  has no files.
- `stage1-controls-5/` and `stage1-placement-6/` (attempts 5 and 6, at
  `b6d9fde`, the first on the recorder staging: each run's streams on a
  private `noswap` tmpfs, published with an inventory and a ledger): the
  controls stopped at the trace buffers' readback before the controls
  workload ran (the allowance ignored the kernel's page rounding); the open
  untraced control ran and was published, and the open timer run stopped at
  the same readback before the probe. Their trace files are startup traces,
  recorded before the stop; no confined run started.
- `stage1-placement-8/` (attempt 8, at `8318db6`): the open untraced control
  and timer run, the timer run as complete as attempt 7's, and the confined
  untraced control, a placement failure: halfway through it a thread of an
  observer, absent from the placement record, was allowed on CPUs 2 and 3,
  and the session stopped before the confined timer run. The placement
  rehearsal was not accepted.
- `rehearsal-10-refresh/` (attempt 10, acquired and analysed at `25eb359`,
  kernel 7.0.0-42-generic, 50 s): failed as a complete rehearsal (reviewer,
  2026-11-10). Its refresh observations passed (`tools/refresh_check.py`), but
  the matcher could not establish an acceptable match at anchor 44370: the
  accepted span ends about 22.75 s into the run, before the refresh at
  47.52 s, and the conversion's coverage fails with it. Its README states the
  verdict and limits; its `diagnostic-what-if-not-evidence/` holds a what-if
  that changes the matcher's input and is never acceptance evidence. The
  reviewer authorized one retry, attempt 11.

**Accepted rehearsals (reviewer, 2026-11-09 and, for attempt 11, 2026-11-10):**

- `stage1-controls-7/` (attempt 7, at `8318db6`), accepted for its recording
  and controls checks: the controls pass; its timer run has zero main-trace
  and TSC-instance losses, zero packets dropped by tcpdump, and complete
  clock, sampler and SMI coverage. Its controls count while the kernel, the
  probe definitions and the event set stay unchanged.
- `stage1-placement-9/` (attempt 9, acquired and analysed at `6933d33`,
  after the repeated observer placement and its read-failure correction),
  accepted as the emulator recording and placement rehearsal within the
  documented ancestry limits: open and confined placements, each an
  untraced control and a timer run. Both timer runs have zero main-trace and
  TSC-instance losses and complete clock, sampler and SMI coverage (the open
  run's conversion excluded one suspect anchor under the existing rule);
  both confined runs kept every attributable observer task off CPUs 2 and 3,
  the untraced one by its midpoint snapshot, the timer one also in its
  trace.
- `rehearsal-11-refresh/` (attempt 11, acquired and analysed at `25eb359`,
  kernel 7.0.0-42-generic, 50 s; accepted by the reviewer on 2026-11-10), the
  focused rehearsal of the sudo refresh's placement and cleanup, within the
  recorded task-attribution limits and the emulator's PTY USB exception: one
  refresh inside the window; its four attributable tasks only off CPUs 2 and
  3; no CPU 3 NULL-page fault; all 97,556 instance anchors paired with no
  suspects; zero losses. It qualifies no hardware control rate.

Every analysis here fails the USB association check by design, the one
exception the acceptances allow: over the pty no USB OUT submission exists,
so the check validates nothing of the USB path (on hardware it stays
strict). All the probe runs fail T1, T2 and T6a, as every emulator run has;
no hardware control rate is qualified by these rehearsals.

The operator quieted the host with `tools/quiet_host.sh` around attempts 3
to 11 (its record is in every session record). The `.correlate.txt` files
are `tools/trace_correlate.py` outputs. Those of attempts 1, 3 and 4 were run
by the operator after each session and saved in the session directories
(they were there before the developer first read them). Those of attempts 7,
8 and 9 were run by the developer (Claude Code) after the session, at
`8318db6` (attempts 7 and 8) and `6933d33` (attempt 9), with `--adapter 1-9`,
and added to the archived folders; the session directories do not hold them.
Those of attempts 10 and 11 were run by the developer after the session at
`25eb359`, unmodified, and are in the archived folders with their resource
use (`.correlate.time.txt`) and the refresh check (`.refresh-check.txt`);
each folder also holds the operator's terminal output, verbatim, and in

## `stage1_hardware/` — control-rate Stage 1 torque-off hardware runs (2026-11-10)

Diagnostic runs on the real arm, every servo torque-off with the owner at the
robot, in three sittings on Linux 7.0.0-42-generic #42. They qualify no control
rate. Each invocation folder keeps its session directory's name and its own
verdict, stated in its README, with the operator's terminal output, the
developer's timestamped evidence review and the analysis the procedure ran. Each
folder holds a `SHA256SUMS` over its files, checked against the copies, and the
copies were compared with the originals, which stay in the operator's session
directories. Described in [control-rate.md](../hardware/control-rate.md).

- **H1, the mechanism reproduction, accepted:** `stage1-mechanism-3/` (attempt 3, at
  `b3979b0`, sitting 1; its STOP was a false count in the procedure, after both
  captures and their publication), `stage1-mechanism-4/` and `stage1-mechanism-5/`
  (attempts 4 and 5, at `6f65c19`, sitting 2).
- **H2, the observer placement comparison, incomplete:**
  - `stage1-placement-6-open/` (attempt 6, at `6f65c19`): the timer run failed
    validity check 3 on two NULL-page kernel faults on CPU 3, from the then unpinned
    sudo refresh. It is kept with its fault-context evidence, its failed verdict
    stands and it does not count toward H2; the untraced run stays valid standalone
    evidence.
  - `stage1-placement-7-open/` (attempt 7, at `5adb1a5`, sitting 3): accepted as valid
    diagnostic evidence.
  - `stage1-placement-7-confined-untraced/` (attempt 7): accepted as independent
    diagnostic evidence within its midpoint-snapshot and ancestry limits.
  - `stage1-placement-7-confined-timer/` (attempt 7): failed validity check 3 and is
    not accepted. The matcher could not establish an acceptable match at anchor
    22810, and the accepted span ends 14.07 s into the run. Its USB, placement and
    cleanup subchecks remain diagnostic evidence only, and its
    `diagnostics-not-acceptance-evidence/` is never acceptance evidence.
- `sittings/` (identity and stop records, the developer's identity reviews, the
  resumed sitting's host provenance), `procedure/` (both procedure versions and the
  developer's read-only gate script), `plan/` (revision 81 as approved) and
  `reviews/` (the reviewer's records).

## `clock_validation/` — clock-alignment emulator validation (2026-11-11)

The clock alignment's emulator validation over `dxl_emu` (emulated servos on a pty: no servo and no USB adapter),
torque-off, on Linux 7.0.0-42-generic, acquired at `2470255`: attempt 12, fresh fair-server controls on CPU 5 and an
untraced control and a timer run at current, 1000 Hz, `rt-loaded-excl`, 20 s; attempt 13, an untraced control and a timer
run at `rt-idle`, 50 s. Emulator data, not hardware telemetry, kept with checksums. The session folders keep their session
directories' names and contents unchanged, each with a `SHA256SUMS`; `analysis-20261111/` holds the developer's derived
analyses with their provenance (the originals unchanged, and corrected analyses added after the review, pending review);
`review/` the reviewer's decision and replay evidence; `README.md` the verdicts; the operator's terminal output is
verbatim. Described in [control-rate.md](../hardware/control-rate.md).

The reviewer split the verdicts (2026-11-11): attempt 12's controls are accepted as fresh controls, and its recorder,
extraction, report, certificate and clock conversion under load as a component demonstration (U about 1,098 ns); the
complete validation has not passed (attempt 12's original analysis fails on 105 worker timer wakes outside every sleep,
and both its probe runs fail T3 with 14 and 3 read failures); attempt 13's certificate is valid but its conversion is
rejected (one local timer entry with no deadline of its own fails the deadline-slot accounting; its 524 ns is not an
accepted bound). The first cost figures are not bounds. No control rate is qualified.

The accounting review (2026-11-11, at `134da46`; `review-accounting/`) accepted the descriptive cost wording and the windowed
counts, supported attempt 12's 105 wakes as guard sleeps by its own replay, and found two corrections: the generic
guard rule (revision 2) did not establish that the write call owned a sleep, and the cost tool's slept cycles were
inferred from deadlines. It approved printing a failed conversion's U as a candidate. `analysis-20261111-rev3/` holds the
corrected analyses (tools at `5d677db`). The re-review (2026-11-11, at `177faee`; `review-rereview/`) accepted them and closed
both findings; its PROVENANCE.txt's "every other figure" excludes the sleep statistics the corrected population moves.
Attempt 12's T3 read failures still prevent an overall validation pass.

The T3 diagnosis (`t3-diagnosis/`, the developer's) was accepted by the reviewer's review (`review-t3/`, at `6db9b3b`), with
a wording correction recorded in README.md: a missing `first_reply_ns` means no completed parsed reply section, not
necessarily no byte. The reviewer chose Option A; `option-a-prep/` holds its new checks' outputs on attempts 12 and 13.
The Option A review (`review-option-a/`, at `d9e774e`) requested changes; `option-a-prep2/` holds the corrected tools'
outputs on the same captures, the corrected commands' dry runs and the reproducers' outcomes. The Option A re-review
(`review-option-a-rereview/`, at `04e8822`) requested changes again; `option-a-prep3/` holds the reviewer's reproducers
and the same cases adapted to the revised interfaces, on the corrected tools (at `6f00c03`). The review at `7553f40`
(`review-option-a-7553f40/`) found two blockers (a `/proc` scan a fork can escape; status files under the caller's
umask); `option-a-prep4/` holds its reproducers and four broken copies' outcomes on the corrected tools (at `2088c43`).
